{"lang_code":"en","pos":"adv","senses":[{"antonyms":[{"word":"a lot"},{"word":"a tad"}],"examples":[{"text":"The door was opened a little.","type":"example"},{"text":"Helping not a little was a squad of volunteers.","type":"example"},{"text":"How are you feeling? ~ A little better, thanks.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To a small extent or degree."],"synonyms":[{"word":"a bit"},{"word":"a little bit"},{"word":"a tad"}],"tags":["not-comparable"]},{"examples":[{"text":"She's a little strange, but nice enough.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Slightly."],"synonyms":[{"word":"a bit"},{"word":"a little"},{"word":"a little bit"},{"word":"a mite"},{"word":"a smidgen"},{"word":"a smidgeon"},{"word":"a tad"},{"tags":["dialectal"],"word":"a touch"},{"word":"a trifle"},{"word":"barely"},{"word":"closely"},{"word":"faintly"},{"word":"hardly"},{"word":"imperceptibly"},{"word":"just"},{"word":"kinda"},{"word":"kinda sorta"},{"tags":["idiomatic"],"word":"kind of"},{"word":"low-key"},{"word":"marginally"},{"word":"mildly"},{"word":"modestly"},{"word":"nearly"},{"word":"only just"},{"word":"scarcely"},{"word":"slightly"},{"word":"somewhat"},{"word":"sorta"},{"word":"sorta kinda"},{"word":"sort of"}],"tags":["not-comparable"]},{"examples":[{"text":"It's a little big, don't you think?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Very."],"tags":["UK","not-comparable"]},{"glosses":["A lot."],"tags":["UK","not-comparable"]},{"examples":[{"text":"It could do with a little work, that's for sure.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A lot of."],"tags":["UK","not-comparable"]}],"sounds":[],"upstream_line":411704,"upstream_offset":1963254295,"upstream_record_sha256":"0e2e3d959ee013de789809d22c7c708e03a94f0e1c53ce1c2d80094a2c30b4e8","word":"a little"}

{"forms":[{"form":"abjads","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["A writing system for Arabic, historically also employed as a numeral system, in which there is one glyph (symbol or letter) for each consonant but vowels are not specified."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Languages that use abjads include Arabic, Hebrew, Persian, and Urdu. Abjads differ from syllabaries (such as the Japanese hiragana) in that the vowel quality of each letter is left unspecified, and must be inferred from context and grammar.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Any writing system in which glyphs are used to represent consonants or consonantal phonemes, but not vowels."],"topics":["human-sciences","linguistics","sciences"]},{"glosses":["The system of abjad numerals; a numeral system in which the letters of the Arabic abjad are interpreted as numerals, typically used to enumerate lists and nested lists, as well as in numerology."]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/ˈæbˌd͡ʒæd/"},{"ipa":"/ˈɑbˌd͡ʒɑd/"}],"synonyms":[{"sense":"writing system with a glyph for each consonant","word":"consonantary"}],"upstream_line":553676,"upstream_offset":2532799099,"upstream_record_sha256":"7d0dfcdb71d1bfae75fb71a4bad5874a30f75a6a2165bd40fa65d4a50d0db835","word":"abjad"}
